The README is a design doc. Below are the exact changes to bring the repo in line with it. Do them in order.

## 1. `package.json`

**Add** these top-level keys (next to `"name"`):
```json
"private": true,
"type": "module",
"engines": { "node": ">=20" },
```

**Add** to `"scripts"` (keep existing; add only what's missing):
```json
"dev": "tsx",
"clean": "rm -rf node_modules dist coverage"
```

**Remove** any script whose body invokes `nodemon`. If a script is named `start` and uses nodemon, replace its body with `"node --watch"` — do not delete the script name.

---

## 2. `tsconfig.base.json`

Ensure `compilerOptions` contains exactly these values (add missing, correct mismatched):

```json
"target": "ES2022",
"module": "NodeNext",
"moduleResolution": "NodeNext",
"strict": true,
"noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
"noImplicitOverride": true,
"exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true,
"verbatimModuleSyntax": true,
"esModuleInterop": true,
"skipLibCheck": true,
"resolveJsonModule": true,
"declaration": true,
"sourceMap": true,
"outDir": "dist",
"rootDir": "src"
```

---

## 3. `tsconfig.json`

**Decision rule:** if it currently has `"include": ["src"]` (or similar), leave it alone. If it's already `files: []`, leave it alone. Only rewrite if it's something else — in which case replace with:

```json
{
  "extends": "./tsconfig.base.json",
  "files": [],
  "include": []
}
```

---

- **Delete `eslint.config.cjs`.**

## Fix

```bash

(If `src/dsa/README.md` already exists with identical content, just delete the root one instead: `git rm README.md`.)

**Then create a new root `README.md`** with the project-level design doc. At minimum it should cover:

1. **What this repo is** — a personal learning/experiments repo for JS/TS, DSA, DB, network, and web-server topics.
2. **Layout** — one line per top-level directory:
   - `src/dsa/` — data structures & algorithms (see `src/dsa/README.md`)
   - `src/es5/`, `src/es6/` — language-version demos (JS, `@ts-nocheck`)
   - `src/ts/` — TypeScript-specific demos
   - `src/db/` — database examples (`sqlite/`, `compose.yaml` for Postgres)
   - `src/network/` — network examples (e.g. `udp-echo/`)
   - `src/web/` — web servers (`servers/raw-node/`, `apps/`)
   - `src/scripts/` — one-off runnable scripts
   - `examples/`, `experiments/`, `playground/` — scratch space (playground is gitignored)
3. **Tooling** — Node ≥20, ESM (`"type": "module"`), TypeScript with `NodeNext`, `tsx` for running, Vitest for tests, ESLint flat config, Prettier.
4. **Scripts** — `dev`, `typecheck`, `test`, `test:watch`, `lint`, `format`, `clean`.
5. **Conventions** — co-located `*.test.ts`, one concept per directory, `// @ts-nocheck` only in `src/es5/` and `src/es6/`, no workspaces yet, no repo-wide `tsc -b`.
6. **Next steps** — the "minimal next steps" list from the design doc.

- Do not duplicate the DSA note in both places.

# Data Structures & Algorithms

One concept per directory. Each directory contains:

- `index.ts` — public re-exports
- `<name>.ts` — implementation
- `<name>.test.ts` — Vitest tests
- `README.md` — notes, complexity, gotchas

README.md
```
# Design

## 1. Purpose

A TypeScript learning repository of data structures and algorithms. Each implementation is small, self-contained, and tested, so it can be read as a reference.

## 2. Scope

In scope:

- Classic data structures (lists, stacks, queues, trees, graphs, heaps, hash tables)
- Common algorithms (sorting, searching, graph traversal, dynamic programming)

Out of scope:

- Production-grade libraries and performance tuning
- Framework or runtime-specific code

## 3. Repository Layout

- `src/dsa/` — all concepts, one directory per concept
- Each concept directory follows the layout in `src/dsa/README.md`
- Tests run with Vitest; dependencies are managed with npm

## 4. Conventions

- Language: TypeScript, with strict type checking enabled
- Public API is exposed only through each directory's `index.ts`
- Implementations favour clarity over cleverness and include complexity notes in their README
- Generated output (`dist/`, coverage reports, caches) is excluded via `.gitignore`

## 5. Testing

- Every concept has a `<name>.test.ts` file alongside its implementation
- Tests cover normal behaviour, edge cases (empty input, single element, duplicates), and invalid input where relevant
- Run the full suite before committing

## 6. Contributing

- Add one concept per directory and keep the structure consistent with existing ones
- Document complexity and gotchas in the concept's `README.md`
- Keep changes focused; one concept or fix per change
```

# Structuring a Multi-Purpose JavaScript/TypeScript Repository

Great idea! A "monorepo playground" is a common and useful pattern. Here's how I'd structure and document it, plus things you likely missed.

---

## 1. Recommended Directory Layout

```
codejs/
├── README.md
├── package.json                 # root (scripts, deps)
├── tsconfig.base.json           # shared TS config
├── .editorconfig
├── .gitignore
├── .nvmrc
├── .prettierrc
├── eslint.config.cjs             # flat config (ESLint 9+)
├── vitest.config.ts             # or jest
│
├── src/                         # main source tree
│   ├── dsa/                     # data structures & algorithms
│   │   ├── structures/
│   │   ├── algorithms/
│   │   └── README.md
│   ├── scripts/                 # general-purpose CLI scripts
│   ├── web/
│   │   ├── servers/             # raw http, express, fastify...
│   │   └── apps/                # small web apps, ui experiments
│   ├── db/                      # postgres, sqlite, mongo, redis...
│   ├── network/                 # tcp, udp, websockets, http clients
│   ├── ts/                      # typescript-specific experiments
│   ├── es6/                     # modern JS features
│   └── es5/                     # legacy JS (var, prototypes, callbacks)
│
├── experiments/                 # throwaway / dated experiments
│   └── 2025-01-foo/
│
├── playground/                  # scratchpad (git-ignored or per-branch)
│
├── examples/                    # runnable, documented examples
│
├── docs/                        # notes, cheatsheets, learnings
│
└── tests/                       # (or co-locate *.test.ts next to source)
```

**Key principle:** everything is TypeScript by default. `.js` files only appear in `es5/` and `es6/` when the *point* is to demonstrate the language version.

---

## 2. Root `package.json` (scripts sketch)

```json
{
  "name": "codejs",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "tsx",
    "typecheck": "tsc --noEmit",
    "test": "vitest run",
    "test:watch": "vitest",
    "lint": "eslint .",
    "format": "prettier --write .",
    "clean": "rm -rf node_modules **/dist"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "typescript": "^5.5.0",
    "vitest": "^2.0.0",
    "tsx": "^4.16.0",
    "eslint": "^9.0.0",
    "prettier": "^3.3.0"
  }
}
```

Use **`tsx`** (or `ts-node` / Node's native `--experimental-strip-types`) to *run* TS files directly without a build step — huge quality-of-life win for a playground.

Add `"workspaces"` or a `"build"` script later, only when you actually need package-per-directory builds or project references.

---

## 3. `README.md` Template

```markdown
# codejs

A personal workspace for learning, experimenting, and building with
JavaScript & TypeScript. Everything is TypeScript by default.

## Quick start

```bash
nvm use            # respects .nvmrc
npm install
npm run typecheck
npm test
npm run dev -- src/scripts/hello.ts   # example
```

## Layout

| Path              | Purpose                                                      |
|-------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| `src/dsa/`        | Data structures & algorithms with tests                     |
| `src/scripts/`    | General-purpose CLI scripts                                 |
| `src/web/`        | Web servers & small web apps                                |
| `src/db/`         | Database experiments (Postgres, SQLite, Mongo, Redis)       |
| `src/network/`    | TCP/UDP/WebSocket/HTTP clients & servers                    |
| `src/ts/`         | TypeScript-only experiments (generics, decorators, types)   |
| `src/es6/`        | Modern JS feature demos                                     |
| `src/es5/`        | Legacy JS demos (var, prototypes, callbacks)                |
| `experiments/`    | Dated, throwaway experiments                                |
| `examples/`       | Polished, runnable examples                                 |
| `docs/`           | Notes, cheatsheets, learnings                               |
| `playground/`     | Local scratch (git-ignored)                                 |

## Conventions

- **TypeScript is the default.** `.js` files only exist in `es5/` / `es6/`
  where the language version *is* the topic.
- Every example is runnable: `npm run dev -- <path>`.
- Algorithms and data structures ship with a `*.test.ts` next to the source.
- New experiments go in `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` with a local README.
- `playground/` is for throwaway code and is `.gitignore`d.

## Tooling

- Node 20+, ESM only, `"type": "module"`
- TypeScript strict mode (`tsconfig.base.json`)
- Vitest for tests, ESLint (flat) + Prettier for style
- `tsx` to run `.ts` directly
```

---

## 4. `tsconfig.base.json`

```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "module": "NodeNext",
    "moduleResolution": "NodeNext",
    "strict": true,
    "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
    "noImplicitOverride": true,
    "exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true,
    "verbatimModuleSyntax": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "skipLibCheck": true,
    "resolveJsonModule": true,
    "declaration": true,
    "sourceMap": true,
    "outDir": "dist",
    "rootDir": "src"
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Things You Might Have Missed

### Repo & workflow hygiene
- **`.nvmrc`** – pin Node version so examples stay reproducible.
- **`"engines": { "node": ">=20" }`** in `package.json`.
- **`.editorconfig` + Prettier** – since it's a learning repo, consistency saves you mental overhead.
- **`.gitattributes`** – line endings, binary paths (e.g. `.db`, `.sqlite` files).
- **`.gitignore`** – `node_modules`, `dist`, `*.sqlite`, `.env*`, `playground/`, coverage.
- **LICENSE** (MIT is fine) – even for personal repos, it makes forks/borrows unambiguous.
- **`.env.example`** – for DB/network experiments you'll eventually need secrets.

### Tooling choices
- **ESM from day one** (`"type": "module"`, `NodeNext`) — the ecosystem is heading there; switching later is painful.
- **Runners**: `tsx` for files, `node --watch` for servers, `vitest` for tests. Pick one and stick to it.
- **Linter**: ESLint v9 flat config (`eslint.config.cjs`) — the old `.eslintrc` is deprecated.
- **Pre-commit hook** via `husky` + `lint-staged` — small cost, keeps the repo clean.
- **`tsc -b` + project references** if you use workspaces; makes `build`/`typecheck` fast.
- **`c8` or Vitest's built-in coverage** — useful for DSA study.

### Repo-specific conventions
- **Naming**: `kebab-case` for files/dirs, `PascalCase` for classes, `camelCase` for functions. Write it down in the README or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- **One concept per directory** in `dsa/` (e.g. `dsa/structures/queue/`) with `index.ts`, `queue.ts`, `queue.test.ts`, `README.md`.
- **Dated experiments**: `experiments/2025-01-09-websocket-chat/` — prevents `experiments/` from becoming a graveyard of `test2.ts`.
- **Archival rule**: promote experiments to `examples/` or `src/` when they become useful; delete otherwise. Say so explicitly in the README.
- **`docs/` for write-ups** — a `learnings.md` per topic; this is where the *learning* actually sticks.

### Things for specific domains
- **`src/db/`**: prefer Docker Compose (`db/compose.yaml`) over local installs; keep `init.sql` / migrations per subproject.
- **`src/web/`**: one folder per stack (`express/`, `fastify/`, `hono/`, `raw-node/`) so comparisons are easy.
- **`src/network/`**: note whether examples bind to `localhost` only; add a `README` with required ports.
- **`src/es5/` and `src/es6/`**: exclude from typechecking if the point is syntax (`// @ts-nocheck` at the top, or a separate `tsconfig` that skips them).
- **`src/ts/`**: keep examples *compile-only* where useful (`tsc --noEmit` showcases) alongside runtime ones.

### Risk/reality checks
- Don't add **workspaces** until you actually have package-per-directory needs — plain folders + root `tsconfig` is simpler.
- Skip a **build step** for the whole repo; use `tsx`/`--watch`. Only `src/scripts` that you actually ship need `tsc -b`.
- Avoid mixing **CJS and ESM** unless the point is to demo it — put such demos in `es5/` or `experiments/`.
- A `playground/` directory that's git-ignored is a great pressure valve; without it, `experiments/` gets polluted.

---

## 6. Minimal Next Steps

1. `npm init -y`, set `"type": "module"`, add the scripts above.
2. Create `tsconfig.base.json` + root `tsconfig.json` (`{ "extends": "./tsconfig.base.json", "files": [] }` with `references` as you grow).
3. Add `.nvmrc`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`, `eslint.config.cjs`, `.prettierrc`, `vitest.config.ts`.
4. Drop the README template in and adjust the table.
5. Write one real example per top-level folder so the layout is self-documenting:
    - `dsa/structures/stack/`
    - `scripts/hello.ts`
    - `web/servers/raw-node/`
    - `db/sqlite/`
    - `network/udp-echo/`
    - `es6/iterators/`
    - `es5/callbacks/`
6. Start using `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` for anything new and prune quarterly.

That's a repo that's pleasant to learn in, easy to grow, and won't fight you when you add the 20th experiment.

# Structuring a Multi-Purpose JavaScript/TypeScript Repository

Great idea! A "monorepo playground" is a common and useful pattern. Here's how I'd structure and document it, plus
things you likely missed.

---

## 1. Recommended Directory Layout

```
codejs/
├── README.md
├── package.json                 # root (scripts, deps)
├── tsconfig.base.json           # shared TS config
├── .editorconfig
├── .gitignore
├── .nvmrc
├── .prettierrc
├── eslint.config.cjs             # flat config (ESLint 9+)
├── vitest.config.ts             # or jest
│
├── src/                         # main source tree
│   ├── dsa/                     # data structures & algorithms
│   │   ├── structures/
│   │   ├── algorithms/
│   │   └── README.md
│   ├── scripts/                 # general-purpose CLI scripts
│   ├── web/
│   │   └── servers/             # raw http, express, fastify...
│   ├── db/                      # postgres, sqlite, mongo, redis...
│   ├── network/                 # tcp, udp, websockets, http clients
│   ├── es6/                     # modern JS features
│   └── es5/                     # legacy JS (var, prototypes, callbacks)
│
├── experiments/                 # throwaway / dated experiments
│   └── 2025-01-foo/
│
├── playground/                  # scratchpad (git-ignored or per-branch)
│
├── examples/                    # runnable, documented examples
│
└── tests/                       # (or co-locate *.test.ts next to source)
```

**Key principle:** everything is TypeScript by default. `.js` files only appear in `es5/` and `es6/` when the *point* is
to demonstrate the language version.

---

## 2. Root `package.json` (scripts sketch)

```json
{
  "name": "codejs",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "scripts": {
    "dev": "tsx",
    "typecheck": "tsc --noEmit",
    "test": "vitest run",
    "test:watch": "vitest",
    "lint": "eslint .",
    "format": "prettier --write .",
    "clean": "rm -rf node_modules **/dist"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "typescript": "^5.5.0",
    "vitest": "^2.0.0",
    "tsx": "^4.16.0",
    "eslint": "^9.0.0",
    "prettier": "^3.3.0"
  }
}
```

Use **`tsx`** (or `ts-node` / Node's native `--experimental-strip-types`) to *run* TS files directly without a build
step — huge quality-of-life win for a playground.

Add `"workspaces"` or a `"build"` script later, only when you actually need package-per-directory builds or project
references.

---

```markdown

## Conventions

- **TypeScript is the default.** `.js` files only exist in `es5/` / `es6/`
  where the language version *is* the topic.
- Every example is runnable: `npm run dev -- <path>`.
- Algorithms and data structures ship with a `*.test.ts` next to the source.
- New experiments go in `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` with a local README.
- `playground/` is for throwaway code and is `.gitignore`d.

## Tooling

- Node 20+, ESM only, `"type": "module"`
- TypeScript strict mode (`tsconfig.base.json`)
- Vitest for tests, ESLint (flat) + Prettier for style
- `tsx` to run `.ts` directly

- Skip a **build step** for the whole repo; use `tsx`/`--watch`. Only `src/scripts` that you actually ship need
  `tsc -b`.
- Avoid mixing **CJS and ESM** unless the point is to demo it — put such demos in `es5/` or `experiments/`.
- A `playground/` directory that's git-ignored is a great pressure valve; without it, `experiments/` gets polluted.

---

## 6. Minimal Next Steps

1. `npm init -y`, set `"type": "module"`, add the scripts above.
2. Create `tsconfig.base.json` + root `tsconfig.json` (`{ "extends": "./tsconfig.base.json", "files": [] }` with
   `references` as you grow).
3. Add `.nvmrc`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`, `eslint.config.cjs`, `.prettierrc`, `vitest.config.ts`.
4. Drop the README template in and adjust the table.
5. Write one real example per top-level folder so the layout is self-documenting:
    - `dsa/structures/stack/`
    - `scripts/hello.ts`
    - `web/servers/raw-node/`
    - `db/sqlite/`
    - `network/udp-echo/`
    - `es6/iterators/`
    - `es5/callbacks/`
6. Start using `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` for anything new and prune quarterly.

That's a repo that's pleasant to learn in, easy to grow, and won't fight you when you add the 20th experiment.
````

src/ts/generics/generics.ts
````
export function identity<T>(value: T): T {
  return value;
}

export function first<T>(items: readonly T[]): T | undefined {
  return items[0];
}
````

src/ts/generics/generics.test.ts
````
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { first, identity } from './generics.js';

describe('identity', () => {
  it('returns the value it was given', () => {
    expect(identity(42)).toBe(42);
    expect(identity('hi')).toBe('hi');
  });
});

describe('first', () => {
  it('returns the first item of a non-empty array', () => {
    expect(first([1, 2, 3])).toBe(1);
  });

  it('returns undefined for an empty array', () => {
    expect(first([])).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
````

src/web/apps/hello/hello.ts
````
import { createServer } from 'node:http';

const PORT = 3000;
const HOST = 'localhost';

const server = createServer((_req, res) => {
  res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain' });
  res.end('hello');
});

server.listen(PORT, HOST, () => {
  console.log(`hello listening on http://${HOST}:${PORT}`);
});
````

src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/binary-search.ts
````
/**
 * Returns the index of `target` in `sorted`, or -1 if it is not present.
 * `sorted` must be in ascending order.
 */
export function binarySearch(sorted: readonly number[], target: number): number {
  let low = 0;
  let high = sorted.length - 1;

  while (low <= high) {
    const mid = low + Math.floor((high - low) / 2);
    const value = sorted[mid];

    if (value === undefined) return -1;
    if (value === target) return mid;

    if (value < target) {
      low = mid + 1;
    } else {
      high = mid - 1;
    }
  }

  return -1;
}
````

src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/binary-search.test.ts
````
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { binarySearch } from './binary-search.js';

describe('binarySearch', () => {
  it('finds a value in the middle', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 5)).toBe(2);
  });

  it('finds the first and last values', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 1)).toBe(0);
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 9)).toBe(4);
  });

  it('returns -1 when the value is absent', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 4)).toBe(-1);
  });

  it('returns -1 for an empty array', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([], 1)).toBe(-1);
  });
});
````

src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/index.ts
````
export * from './binary-search.js';
````

src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/README.md
````
# Binary search

Finds a target value in a sorted array by repeatedly halving the search range.

## Complexity

| Case    | Time     | Space    |
|---------|----------|----------|
| Best    | O(1)     | O(1)     |
| Average | O(log n) | O(1)     |
| Worst   | O(log n) | O(1)     |

## Notes

- Input must be sorted in ascending order; otherwise the result is undefined.
- The iterative version used here avoids the call-stack growth of a recursive one.
- `mid` is computed as `low + (high - low) / 2` to avoid integer overflow in other languages.
````

docs/cheatsheets.md
````
# Cheatsheets

## git

- `git status` – show working tree state
- `git switch -c <branch>` – create and switch to a branch
- `git stash push -m "msg"` – stash changes with a message

## npm

- `npm install` – install dependencies from `package.json`
- `npm run <script>` – run a script defined in `package.json`
- `npm run dev -- <file>` – run a TypeScript file with `tsx`

## tsconfig

- `strict` – enables the full set of strict type-checking options
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` – array index access returns `T | undefined`
- `verbatimModuleSyntax` – requires `import type` for type-only imports
````

# Learnings

A running log of what I learned while working in this repo. Add a section per topic.

## Template

### Topic

- What I tried:
- What happened:
- What I'd do differently:
````

CONTRIBUTING.md
````
# Contributing

This is a personal learning repo, but these conventions keep it consistent.

## Naming

- Files and directories: `kebab-case` (e.g. `binary-search.ts`)
- Classes: `PascalCase`
- Functions and variables: `camelCase`

## Structure

- One concept per directory in `dsa/` (e.g. `dsa/structures/queue/`) with `index.ts`, the implementation, its test, and a `README.md`.
- Dated experiments go in `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` with a short local README.
- Promote experiments to `examples/` or `src/` when they become useful; delete them otherwise.

## Workflow

- Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, and `npm run lint` before committing.
- The pre-commit hook runs `lint-staged`, which formats staged files with Prettier.
````

Notes on the deviations and what I left alone:

- **Root `tsconfig.json`:** `files: []` with `references: []` makes `tsc --noEmit` fail with "No inputs were found" (TS18003), so I added a reference to `src/`. `src/tsconfig.json` is the single composite project that covers `src/ts`, `src/web/apps`, `src/dsa`, and `src/scripts`. Add more references as you grow.
- **`typecheck` script:** changed from `tsc --noEmit` to `tsc -b`, since the solution-style root needs `-b` to check anything.
- **`dev` script:** added `"dev": "tsx"`. The README uses `npm run dev -- <path>` throughout, but the previous `package.json` had no `dev` script.
- **ESLint:** `npm run lint` needs `eslint.config.cjs`, so I added a minimal flat config with `typescript-eslint` and `@eslint/js`.
- **Workspaces risk:** npm expects each workspace glob match to contain a `package.json`. `experiments/2025-01-hello/` and any `src/*` directory without one may cause `npm install` to error or warn. If that happens, remove the `workspaces` field, as README §5 suggests until you need per-package dependencies.
- **Husky:** `.husky/pre-commit` needs to be executable (`chmod +x .husky/pre-commit`). The `prepare` script only installs the hook if the project is a git repository.
- **README:** I left it unchanged. The version in the chat already contains the §1 tree entries, the §3 layout and template items, the `## Related` section, the §5 husky and coverage bullets, the `CONTRIBUTING.md` mention, and the §6 pre-commit step, so there was nothing to restore.

### 1. `src/dsa/algorithms/` — needs at least one real example

Create `src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/`:

**`src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/binary-search.ts`**
```ts
export function binarySearch(sorted: readonly number[], target: number): number {
  let low = 0;
  let high = sorted.length - 1;

  while (low <= high) {
    const mid = low + Math.floor((high - low) / 2);
    const value = sorted[mid];

    if (value === undefined) return -1;
    if (value === target) return mid;
    if (value < target) low = mid + 1;
    else high = mid - 1;
  }

  return -1;
}
```

**`src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/binary-search.test.ts`**
```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { binarySearch } from './binary-search.js';

describe('binarySearch', () => {
  it('returns -1 for empty array', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([], 1)).toBe(-1);
  });

  it('finds an element', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 5)).toBe(2);
  });

  it('returns -1 when missing', () => {
    expect(binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 4)).toBe(-1);
  });
});
```

**`src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/index.ts`**
```ts

```

import { createServer } from 'node:http';

const port = Number(process.env.PORT ?? 3000);

const server = createServer((_req, res) => {
  res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/plain' });
  res.end('hello\n');
});

server.listen(port, 'localhost', () => {
  console.log(`listening on http://localhost:${port}`);
});
```

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { first, identity } from './generics.js';

});

# Binary Search

Returns the index of a target value in a sorted array, or `-1` if the value is not present.

## Usage

```ts
import { binarySearch } from './binary-search.js';

binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 5); // 2
binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 4); // -1
binarySearch([], 1);              // -1
```

## Notes

- The input array must be sorted in ascending order. Results are undefined otherwise.
- Runs in O(log n) time and O(1) space.

## Tests

Tests live next to the source in `binary-search.test.ts`:

```bash
npm test
```
````

src/web/apps/hello/README.md
````
# Hello Web App

A minimal Node.js HTTP server that responds to every request with `hello`.

## Run

```bash
npx tsx src/web/apps/hello/hello.ts
```

Then open http://localhost:3000 in a browser or run:

```bash
curl http://localhost:3000
```

## Details

- Listens on `localhost:3000` only.
- Uses the built-in `node:http` module, with no framework dependencies.
- Returns `200 OK` with a `text/plain` body.
````

src/ts/generics/README.md
````
# Generics

Small examples of TypeScript generic functions.

## Contents

- `identity<T>(value: T): T` returns the value it was given, unchanged.
- `first<T>(items: readonly T[]): T | undefined` returns the first item of an array, or `undefined` if the array is empty.

## Usage

```ts
import { first, identity } from './index.js';

identity(42);           // 42
first(['a', 'b']);      // 'a'
first([]);              // undefined
```

`first` returns `T | undefined` rather than `T` because an empty array has no first item. The type system makes callers handle that case.

## Tests

Tests live next to the source in `generics.test.ts`:

```bash
npm test
```
````

CONVENTIONS.md
````
# Structuring a Multi-Purpose JavaScript/TypeScript Repository

Great idea! A "monorepo playground" is a common and useful pattern. Here's how I'd structure and document it, plus things you likely missed.

---

## 1. Recommended Directory Layout

```
codejs/
├── README.md
├── package.json                 # root (scripts; workspaces deferred, see §5)
├── tsconfig.base.json           # shared TS config
├── .editorconfig
├── .gitignore
├── .nvmrc
├── .prettierrc
├── eslint.config.cjs             # flat config (ESLint 9+)
├── vitest.config.ts             # or jest
│
├── src/                         # main source tree
│   ├── dsa/                     # data structures & algorithms
│   │   ├── structures/
│   │   ├── algorithms/
│   │   └── README.md
│   ├── scripts/                 # general-purpose CLI scripts
│   ├── web/
│   │   ├── servers/             # raw http, express, fastify...
│   │   └── apps/                # small web apps, ui experiments
│   ├── db/                      # postgres, sqlite, mongo, redis...
│   ├── network/                 # tcp, udp, websockets, http clients
│   ├── ts/                      # typescript-specific experiments
│   ├── es6/                     # modern JS features
│   └── es5/                     # legacy JS (var, prototypes, callbacks)
│
├── experiments/                 # throwaway / dated experiments
│   └── 2025-01-foo/
│
├── playground/                  # scratchpad (git-ignored or per-branch)
│
├── examples/                    # runnable, documented examples
│
├── docs/                        # notes, cheatsheets, learnings
│
└── tests/                       # (or co-locate *.test.ts next to source)
```

**Key principle:** everything is TypeScript by default. `.js` files only appear in `es5/` and `es6/` when the *point* is to demonstrate the language version.

---

## 2. Root `package.json` (sketch)

Workspaces are intentionally omitted until they're needed (see §5). `typecheck` uses `tsc --noEmit` against the root `tsconfig.json`.

```json
{
  "name": "codejs",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "scripts": {
    "build": "tsc -b",
    "typecheck": "tsc --noEmit",
    "test": "vitest run",
    "test:watch": "vitest",
    "lint": "eslint .",
    "format": "prettier --write .",
    "clean": "rm -rf node_modules **/dist"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "typescript": "^5.5.0",
    "vitest": "^2.0.0",
    "tsx": "^4.16.0",
    "eslint": "^9.0.0",
    "prettier": "^3.3.0"
  }
}
```

Use **`tsx`** (or `ts-node` / Node's native `--experimental-strip-types`) to *run* TS files directly without a build step — huge quality-of-life win for a playground.

---

## 3. `README.md` Template

```markdown
# codejs

A personal workspace for learning, experimenting, and building with
JavaScript & TypeScript. Everything is TypeScript by default.

## Quick start

```bash
nvm use            # respects .nvmrc
npm install
npm run typecheck
npm test
npm run dev -- src/web/apps/hello   # example
```

## Layout

| Path              | Purpose                                                      |
|-------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------|
| `src/dsa/`        | Data structures & algorithms with tests                     |
| `src/scripts/`    | General-purpose CLI scripts                                 |
| `src/web/`        | Web servers & small web apps                                |
| `src/db/`         | Database experiments (Postgres, SQLite, Mongo, Redis)       |
| `src/network/`    | TCP/UDP/WebSocket/HTTP clients & servers                    |
| `src/ts/`         | TypeScript-only experiments (generics, decorators, types)   |
| `src/es6/`        | Modern JS feature demos                                     |
| `src/es5/`        | Legacy JS demos (var, prototypes, callbacks)                |
| `experiments/`    | Dated, throwaway experiments                                |
| `examples/`       | Polished, runnable examples                                 |
| `docs/`           | Notes, cheatsheets, learnings                               |
| `playground/`     | Local scratch (git-ignored)                                 |

## Conventions

- **TypeScript is the default.** `.js` files only exist in `es5/` / `es6/`
  where the language version *is* the topic.
- Every example is runnable: `npm run dev -- <path>`.
- Algorithms and data structures ship with a `*.test.ts` next to the source.
- New experiments go in `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` with a local README.
- `playground/` is for throwaway code and is `.gitignore`d.

## Tooling

- Node 20+, ESM only, `"type": "module"`
- TypeScript strict mode (`tsconfig.base.json`)
- Vitest for tests, ESLint (flat) + Prettier for style
- `tsx` to run `.ts` directly

## Related

- [docs/cheatsheets.md](docs/cheatsheets.md)
```

---

## 4. `tsconfig.base.json`

```json
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "module": "NodeNext",
    "moduleResolution": "NodeNext",
    "strict": true,
    "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
    "noImplicitOverride": true,
    "exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true,
    "verbatimModuleSyntax": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "skipLibCheck": true,
    "resolveJsonModule": true,
    "declaration": true,
    "sourceMap": true,
    "outDir": "dist",
    "rootDir": "src"
  }
}
```

---

## 5. Things You Might Have Missed

### Repo & workflow hygiene
- **`.nvmrc`** – pin Node version so examples stay reproducible.
- **`"engines": { "node": ">=20" }`** in `package.json`.
- **`.editorconfig` + Prettier** – since it's a learning repo, consistency saves you mental overhead.
- **`.gitattributes`** – line endings, binary paths (e.g. `.db`, `.sqlite` files).
- **`.gitignore`** – `node_modules`, `dist`, `*.sqlite`, `.env*`, `playground/`, coverage.
- **LICENSE** (MIT is fine) – even for personal repos, it makes forks/borrows unambiguous.
- **`.env.example`** – for DB/network experiments you'll eventually need secrets.

### Tooling choices
- **ESM from day one** (`"type": "module"`, `NodeNext`) — the ecosystem is heading there; switching later is painful.
- **Runners**: `tsx` for files, `node --watch` for servers, `vitest` for tests. Pick one and stick to it.
- **Linter**: ESLint v9 flat config (`eslint.config.cjs`) — the old `.eslintrc` is deprecated.
- **Pre-commit hook** via `husky` + `lint-staged` — small cost, keeps the repo clean.
- **`tsc -b` + project references** if you use workspaces; makes `build`/`typecheck` fast.
- **`c8` or Vitest's built-in coverage** — useful for DSA study.

### Repo-specific conventions
- **Naming**: `kebab-case` for files/dirs, `PascalCase` for classes, `camelCase` for functions. Write it down in the README or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- **One concept per directory** in `dsa/` (e.g. `dsa/structures/queue/`) with `index.ts`, `queue.ts`, `queue.test.ts`, `README.md`.
- **Dated experiments**: `experiments/2025-01-09-websocket-chat/` — prevents `experiments/` from becoming a graveyard of `test2.ts`.
- **Archival rule**: promote experiments to `examples/` or `src/` when they become useful; delete otherwise. Say so explicitly in the README.
- **`docs/` for write-ups** — a `learnings.md` per topic; this is where the *learning* actually sticks.

### Things for specific domains
- **`src/db/`**: prefer Docker Compose (`db/compose.yaml`) over local installs; keep `init.sql` / migrations per subproject.
- **`src/web/`**: one folder per stack (`express/`, `fastify/`, `hono/`, `raw-node/`) so comparisons are easy.
- **`src/network/`**: note whether examples bind to `localhost` only; add a `README` with required ports.
- **`src/es5/` and `src/es6/`**: exclude from typechecking if the point is syntax (`// @ts-nocheck` at the top, or a separate `tsconfig` that skips them).
- **`src/ts/`**: keep examples *compile-only* where useful (`tsc --noEmit` showcases) alongside runtime ones.

### Risk/reality checks
- Don't add **workspaces** until you actually have package-per-directory needs — plain folders + root `tsconfig` is simpler.
- Skip a **build step** for the whole repo; use `tsx`/`--watch`. Only `src/scripts` that you actually ship need `tsc -b`.
- Avoid mixing **CJS and ESM** unless the point is to demo it — put such demos in `es5/` or `experiments/`.
- A `playground/` directory that's git-ignored is a great pressure valve; without it, `experiments/` gets polluted.

---

## 6. Minimal Next Steps

1. `npm init -y`, set `"type": "module"`, add the scripts above.
2. Create `tsconfig.base.json` + root `tsconfig.json` (`{ "extends": "./tsconfig.base.json", "files": [] }` with `references` as you grow).
3. Add `.nvmrc`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitignore`, `LICENSE`, `eslint.config.cjs`, `.prettierrc`, `vitest.config.ts`.
4. Drop the README template in and adjust the table.
5. Write one real example per top-level folder so the layout is self-documenting:
    - `dsa/structures/stack/`
    - `scripts/hello.ts`
    - `web/servers/raw-node/`
    - `db/sqlite/`
    - `network/udp-echo/`
    - `es6/iterators/`
    - `es5/callbacks/`
6. Add a pre-commit hook.
7. Start using `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` for anything new and prune quarterly.

That's a repo that's pleasant to learn in, easy to grow, and won't fight you when you add the 20th experiment.
````

src/dsa/algorithms/binary-search/README.md
````
# Binary Search

Finds the index of a target value in a sorted array in O(log n) time and O(1) space.

## Usage

```ts
import { binarySearch } from './binary-search.js';

binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 5); // 2
binarySearch([1, 3, 5, 7, 9], 4); // -1
```

`sorted` must be in ascending order. The function returns the index of `target`, or `-1` if it is not present.

## Approach

1. Keep a search window `[low, high]`, starting with the whole array.
2. Check the middle element.
3. If it equals the target, return its index.
4. If it is smaller than the target, search the right half. Otherwise, search the left half.
5. Stop when the window is empty and return `-1`.

## Tests

Run from the repo root:

```bash
npm test
```

# Plan: Align repo with README.md structure

The repo already has most of the README's layout. The gaps are: missing top-level folders (`examples/`, `docs/`, `playground/`), missing `src/` subfolders (`db/`, `network/`, `es6/`, `es5/`, `web/servers/`), missing root config files (`.nvmrc`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitattributes`, `LICENSE`, `.env.example`), a stale `eslint.config.cjs` that conflicts with the flat config, and stale nodemon files that aren't referenced by any script.

Below are the exact changes.

---

## 1. Delete stale files

Delete these files — they are not referenced by `package.json` scripts and conflict with the README's tooling choices (ESLint 9 flat config, `tsx` runner):

- `eslint.config.cjs` — superseded by `eslint.config.cjs` (ESLint 9 flat config). Keeping both causes ESLint to warn/error.
- `nodemon.json` — not referenced by any script; README specifies `tsx` + `node --watch`, not nodemon.
- `nodemon-js.json` — same reason.
- `nodemon-exec.sh` — same reason.

---

## 2. Create missing top-level directories (with `.gitkeep`)

Create these directories, each with an empty `.gitkeep` file so git tracks them:

- `examples/.gitkeep`
- `docs/.gitkeep` (or move existing `docs/cheatsheets.md` / `docs/learnings.md` here if they exist — they're already listed in the repo summary, so just ensure the folder exists)
- `playground/.gitkeep` — **but** `playground/` is git-ignored per `.gitignore`, so do **not** add `.gitkeep`; instead just create the folder locally. Skip this one in git.

Create these `src/` subdirectories, each with a `.gitkeep`:

- `src/db/.gitkeep` (note: `src/db/compose.yaml` already exists per the summary, so this folder is already tracked — skip `.gitkeep`)
- `src/network/.gitkeep`
- `src/es6/.gitkeep`
- `src/es5/.gitkeep`
- `src/web/servers/.gitkeep`

---

## 3. Create root config files

### `.nvmrc`
```
20
```

### `.editorconfig`
```
root = true

[*]
charset = utf-8
end_of_line = lf
insert_final_newline = true
indent_style = tab
indent_size = 2
trim_trailing_whitespace = true

[*.md]
trim_trailing_whitespace = false
```

### `.gitattributes`
```
* text=auto eol=lf
*.sqlite binary
*.db binary
```

### `LICENSE`
MIT license text, copyright line: `Copyright (c) 2025 <your name>`.

### `.env.example`
```
# Copy to .env and fill in. Never commit .env.
# DATABASE_URL=postgres://user:pass@localhost:5432/db
# REDIS_URL=redis://localhost:6379
```

---

## 4. Update `.gitignore`

Add these entries (append to the existing file, do not remove anything):

```
# Playground scratchpad (already present — keep)
playground/

# Env files (already present — keep)
.env
.env.test
```

The existing `.gitignore` already covers `playground/`, `.env`, `dist`, `node_modules`, `*.sqlite`, `*.db`, and `!.env.example`. **No change needed** unless you want to also ignore `experiments/*/node_modules` — optional, skip for now.

---

## 5. Update `package.json`

Two changes:

**a)** Add a `dev` script that matches the README's `npm run dev -- <path>` convention. The current `"dev": "tsx"` already works for this (`npm run dev -- src/scripts/hello.ts`), so **no change needed** — leave it.

**b)** Add `"engines"` — already present (`>=20`). **No change.**

**c)** Add a `lint:fix` script for convenience (optional but matches README's tooling section):

In `"scripts"`, add:
```json
"lint:fix": "eslint . --fix",
```

---

## 6. Update `tsconfig.json` (root)

The current root `tsconfig.json` references `./src`, which is correct. **No change needed.**

However, `src/tsconfig.json` has `"rootDir": "."` and `"outDir": "../dist/src"` — this is fine for the composite build. **No change needed.**

---

## 7. Update `vitest.config.ts`

The current config only includes `src/**/*.test.ts`. The README says tests live next to source in `src/`, `examples/`, and `experiments/`. Update the `include` array:

```ts
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';

export default defineConfig({
	test: {
		include: [
			'src/**/*.test.ts',
			'examples/**/*.test.ts',
			'experiments/**/*.test.ts',
		],
	},
});
```

---

## 8. Update `eslint.config.cjs`

The current flat config ignores `dist`, `node_modules`, `coverage`. Add `playground/` and `experiments/` (experiments are throwaway and may not pass lint):

```js
import js from '@eslint/js';
import tseslint from 'typescript-eslint';

export default tseslint.config(
  { ignores: ['**/dist/**', 'node_modules/**', 'coverage/**', 'playground/**', 'experiments/**'] },
  js.configs.recommended,
  ...tseslint.configs.recommended,
);
```

---

## 9. Update `CONTRIBUTING.md`

Add a short section on the new folders so contributors know where things go. Append:

```markdown
## Where things go

- `src/dsa/` — data structures & algorithms (with tests)
- `src/scripts/` — general-purpose CLI scripts
- `src/web/servers/` — HTTP/TCP servers (raw-node, express, fastify, hono)
- `src/web/apps/` — small web apps / UI experiments
- `src/db/` — database experiments (Postgres, SQLite, Mongo, Redis)
- `src/network/` — TCP/UDP/WebSocket/HTTP clients & servers
- `src/ts/` — TypeScript-only experiments (generics, decorators, types)
- `src/es6/` — modern JS feature demos
- `src/es5/` — legacy JS demos (var, prototypes, callbacks)
- `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` — dated, throwaway experiments
- `examples/` — polished, runnable examples
- `docs/` — notes, cheatsheets, learnings
- `playground/` — local scratch (git-ignored)
```

---

## 10. Move existing files (optional, recommended)

These are optional but align with the README's "one concept per directory" rule:

- `src/web/apps/hello/hello.ts` → keep as-is (already in the right place).
- `src/scripts/hello.ts` → keep as-is.
- `experiments/2025-01-hello/hello.ts` → keep as-is (already dated correctly).

**No moves required.**

---

## 11. Verify

After the changes, run:

```bash
npm run typecheck
npm test
npm run lint
```

All three should pass. If `npm run lint` complains about `eslint.config.cjs` being present, confirm it was deleted in step 1.

---

| Delete | `nodemon.json` |
| Delete | `nodemon-js.json` |

export default defineConfig({
	test: {
		include: [
			'src/**/*.test.ts',
			'examples/**/*.test.ts',
			'experiments/**/*.test.ts',
		],

eslint.config.cjs
````

import tseslint from 'typescript-eslint';

export default tseslint.config(
  { ignores: ['**/dist/**', 'node_modules/**', 'coverage/**', 'playground/**', 'experiments/**'] },
  js.configs.recommended,
  ...tseslint.configs.recommended,
);

CONTRIBUTING.md
````
# Contributing

This is a personal learning repo, but these conventions keep it consistent.

## Naming

- Files and directories: `kebab-case` (e.g. `binary-search.ts`)
- Classes: `PascalCase`
- Functions and variables: `camelCase`

## Structure

- One concept per directory in `dsa/` (e.g. `dsa/structures/queue/`) with `index.ts`, the implementation, its test, and a `README.md`.
- Dated experiments go in `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` with a short local README.
- Promote experiments to `examples/` or `src/` when they become useful; delete them otherwise.

## Workflow

- Run `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, and `npm run lint` before committing.
- The pre-commit hook runs `lint-staged`, which formats staged files with Prettier.

## Where things go

- `src/dsa/` — data structures & algorithms (with tests)
- `src/scripts/` — general-purpose CLI scripts
- `src/web/servers/` — HTTP/TCP servers (raw-node, express, fastify, hono)
- `src/web/apps/` — small web apps / UI experiments
- `src/db/` — database experiments (Postgres, SQLite, Mongo, Redis)
- `src/network/` — TCP/UDP/WebSocket/HTTP clients & servers
- `src/ts/` — TypeScript-only experiments (generics, decorators, types)
- `src/es6/` — modern JS feature demos
- `src/es5/` — legacy JS demos (var, prototypes, callbacks)
- `experiments/YYYY-MM-topic/` — dated, throwaway experiments
- `examples/` — polished, runnable examples
- `docs/` — notes, cheatsheets, learnings
- `playground/` — local scratch (git-ignored)
````

**Important:** this requires `typescript-eslint` as a devDependency. The request's devDependency list does not include it. Two options:

- **Option A (recommended):** add `"typescript-eslint": "^8.0.0"` to `devDependencies` in `package.json` (section 1). This is the only way the flat config will parse `.ts` files.
- **Option B:** if you truly want to omit it, replace the file with a plain JS-only config:

```js

I recommend Option A — the request explicitly says "TypeScript parser", which requires `typescript-eslint`.

import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    include: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'examples/**/*.test.ts', 'experiments/**/*.test.ts'],
    coverage: {
      enabled: true,
      provider: 'v8',
      reporter: ['text', 'html'],
      include: ['src/**/*.ts'],
      exclude: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'src/**/index.ts'],
    },
  },
});
```

**Important:** `provider: 'v8'` requires `@vitest/coverage-v8` as a devDependency. The request's devDependency list does not include it. Add `"@vitest/coverage-v8": "^2.0.0"` to `devDependencies` in section 1, otherwise `npm test` will fail with "Cannot find dependency '@vitest/coverage-v8'".

## 10. `README.md` (overwrite)

# codejs

A personal learning workspace for JavaScript and TypeScript experiments.

TypeScript is the default language for everything in this repo. `.js` files
only appear where the point is to demonstrate a specific JS language version.

See [CONVENTIONS.md](./CONVENTIONS.md) for coding rules, file layout, and
the do-not-touch list.

## Quick start

```bash
nvm use
npm install
npm run typecheck
npm test
```
```

## 12. Delete stale files

Delete these — they are not referenced by the new `package.json` and conflict with the new tooling:

- `eslint.config.cjs` — superseded by `eslint.config.cjs` (ESLint 9 flat config).
- `nodemon.json` — not referenced; `dev` script uses `tsx watch`.
- `nodemon-js.json` — same.
- `nodemon-exec.sh` — same.
- `.husky/pre-commit` — `husky` is no longer a devDependency and `prepare` script is gone. Delete the `.husky/` directory entirely.

---

## 13. Verification (mental)

After creating the files:

- `npm install` will install the 6 devDependencies (plus `typescript-eslint` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` if you take the recommended additions in sections 8 and 9).
- `npx tsc --noEmit` will parse `tsconfig.json` → `tsconfig.base.json`. Because `files: []` and `references: []` are both empty, `tsc` will succeed with no output (nothing to compile). This is expected — the config is valid but there are no source files yet.
- `npm run lint` will run ESLint over the repo. With `typescript-eslint` installed, it will lint `.ts` files (none yet) and `.js` files (`eslint.config.cjs` itself). With Option B, it will only lint `.js` files.
- `npm test` will run Vitest with coverage enabled. With no test files, Vitest exits 0 with "No test files found" (or fails depending on version — if it fails, that's expected until the first test is added).

---

#### ];

#### I recommend Option A — the request explicitly says "TypeScript parser", which requires `typescript-eslint`.

#### ## 13. Verification (mental)

#### After creating the files:  
####   
#### - `npm install` will install the 6 devDependencies (plus `typescript-eslint` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` if you take the recommended additions in sections 8 and 9).  
#### - `npx tsc --noEmit` will parse `tsconfig.json` → `tsconfig.base.json`. Because `files: []` and `references: []` are both empty, `tsc` will succeed with no output (nothing to compile). This is expected — the config is valid but there are no source files yet.  
#### - `npm run lint` will run ESLint over the repo. With `typescript-eslint` installed, it will lint `.ts` files (none yet) and `.js` files (`eslint.config.cjs` itself). With Option B, it will only lint `.js` files.  
#### - `npm test` will run Vitest with coverage enabled. With no test files, Vitest exits 0 with "No test files found" (or fails depending on version — if it fails, that's expected until the first test is added).

Here are the overwritten files. I added `typescript-eslint` and `@vitest/coverage-v8` to `devDependencies` because the ESLint and Vitest configs require them.

package.json
````
{
  "name": "codejs",
  "version": "0.1.0",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "engines": {
    "node": ">=20"
  },
  "scripts": {
    "build": "tsc -b",
    "typecheck": "tsc --noEmit",
    "test": "vitest run",
    "test:watch": "vitest",
    "lint": "eslint .",
    "format": "prettier --write .",
    "dev": "tsx watch"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "@types/node": "^20.14.0",
    "@vitest/coverage-v8": "^2.0.0",
    "eslint": "^9.0.0",
    "prettier": "^3.3.0",
    "tsx": "^4.16.0",
    "typescript": "^5.5.0",
    "typescript-eslint": "^8.0.0",
    "vitest": "^2.0.0"
  }
}
````

tsconfig.base.json
````
{
  "compilerOptions": {
    "target": "ES2022",
    "module": "NodeNext",
    "moduleResolution": "NodeNext",
    "strict": true,
    "noUncheckedIndexedAccess": true,
    "noImplicitOverride": true,
    "exactOptionalPropertyTypes": true,
    "verbatimModuleSyntax": true,
    "esModuleInterop": true,
    "skipLibCheck": true,
    "resolveJsonModule": true,
    "declaration": true,
    "sourceMap": true,
    "outDir": "dist",
    "rootDir": "src"
  }
}
````

tsconfig.json
````
{
  "extends": "./tsconfig.base.json",
  "files": [],
  "references": []
}
````

````
20
````

root = true

[*]
charset = utf-8
end_of_line = lf

[*.md]

dist/
coverage/

````

eslint.config.cjs
````
import tseslint from 'typescript-eslint';

export default tseslint.config(
  { ignores: ['**/dist/**', 'node_modules/**', 'coverage/**', 'playground/**'] },
  ...tseslint.configs.recommended,
);
````

vitest.config.ts
````
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';

export default defineConfig({
  test: {
    include: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'examples/**/*.test.ts', 'experiments/**/*.test.ts'],
    coverage: {
      enabled: true,
      provider: 'v8',
      reporter: ['text', 'html'],
      include: ['src/**/*.ts'],
      exclude: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'src/**/index.ts'],
    },
  },
});
````

README.md
````
# codejs

A personal learning workspace for JavaScript and TypeScript experiments.

TypeScript is the default language for everything in this repo. `.js` files
only appear where the point is to demonstrate a specific JS language version.

See [CONVENTIONS.md](./CONVENTIONS.md) for coding rules, file layout, and
the do-not-touch list.

## Quick start

```bash
nvm use
npm install
npm run typecheck
npm test
```
````

/**

```

```markdown
# Data structures

Stacks, queues, linked lists, trees, graphs, heaps, tries, and friends.

Each structure lives in its own subdirectory (e.g. `queue/`) with an
implementation, a sibling `*.test.ts`, and a short `README.md`.

Run tests: `npm test -- src/dsa/structures`
```

Postgres, SQLite, Mongo, Redis, and friends.

Start services: `docker compose -f src/db/compose.yaml up -d`
```

Run one with: `node src/es6/<name>.js`
```

# Instructions: Create the Stack data structure

Create the four files below. Do not modify any other files.

---

## `src/dsa/structures/stack/stack.ts`

```ts
/**
 * A generic LIFO stack backed by an array.
 */
export class Stack<T> {
  private readonly items: T[] = [];

  push(item: T): void {
    this.items.push(item);
  }

  pop(): T {
    const item = this.items.pop();
    if (item === undefined) {
      throw new Error('Stack is empty');
    }
    return item;
  }

  peek(): T {
    const item = this.items[this.items.length - 1];
    if (item === undefined) {
      throw new Error('Stack is empty');
    }
    return item;
  }

  isEmpty(): boolean {
    return this.items.length === 0;
  }

  size(): number {
    return this.items.length;
  }
}
```

Note on `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`: `this.items[this.items.length - 1]` is typed `T | undefined`, so the `=== undefined` guard is required and correct. `Array.prototype.pop` already returns `T | undefined`, so the same guard applies there.

---

## `src/dsa/structures/stack/stack.test.ts`

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { Stack } from './stack.js';

describe('Stack', () => {
  it('is empty when created', () => {
    const stack = new Stack<number>();
    expect(stack.isEmpty()).toBe(true);
    expect(stack.size()).toBe(0);
  });

  it('pushes and pops in LIFO order', () => {
    const stack = new Stack<number>();
    stack.push(1);
    stack.push(2);
    stack.push(3);
    expect(stack.size()).toBe(3);
    expect(stack.pop()).toBe(3);
    expect(stack.pop()).toBe(2);
    expect(stack.pop()).toBe(1);
    expect(stack.isEmpty()).toBe(true);
  });

  it('peeks at the top without removing it', () => {
    const stack = new Stack<string>();
    stack.push('a');
    stack.push('b');
    expect(stack.peek()).toBe('b');
    expect(stack.size()).toBe(2);
  });

  it('throws when popping from an empty stack', () => {
    const stack = new Stack<number>();
    expect(() => stack.pop()).toThrow('Stack is empty');
  });

  it('throws when peeking at an empty stack', () => {
    const stack = new Stack<number>();
    expect(() => stack.peek()).toThrow('Stack is empty');
  });
});
```

- `pop` and `peek` throw when the stack is empty
- All operations are O(1) amortized

/**
 * A generic LIFO stack backed by an array.
 */
export class Stack<T> {
  private readonly items: T[] = [];

  push(item: T): void {
    this.items.push(item);
  }

  pop(): T {
    const item = this.items.pop();
    if (item === undefined) {
      throw new Error('Stack is empty');
    }
    return item;
  }

  peek(): T {
    const item = this.items[this.items.length - 1];
    if (item === undefined) {
      throw new Error('Stack is empty');
    }
    return item;
  }

  isEmpty(): boolean {
    return this.items.length === 0;
  }

  size(): number {
    return this.items.length;
  }
}
````

## `src/web/servers/raw-node/server.ts`

```ts
import { createServer, type IncomingMessage, type Server, type ServerResponse } from 'node:http';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

function handler(req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse): void {
  if (req.method === 'GET') {
    const path = req.url ?? '/';
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
    res.end(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, path }));
    return;
  }

  res.writeHead(404, { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' });
  res.end(JSON.stringify({ ok: false, error: 'not found' }));
}

export function start(port = 3000): Server {
  const server = createServer(handler);
  server.listen(port, () => {
    console.log(`raw-node server listening on http://localhost:${port}`);
  });
  return server;
}

const isMain = process.argv[1] !== undefined && fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1];

if (isMain) {
  start();
}
```

Notes on the constraints:

- `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on, so `IncomingMessage`, `Server`, and `ServerResponse` are imported with `import type` (inline `type` modifier).
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on, so `process.argv[1]` is `string | undefined` — the `!== undefined` guard is required.
- `import.meta.url` is a `file://` URL; `fileURLToPath` converts it to a path for comparison with `process.argv[1]`. This is the ESM-safe "run directly" check.
- `req.url` is `string | undefined` under `strict`; the `?? '/'` fallback handles it.

import { createServer, type IncomingMessage, type Server, type ServerResponse } from 'node:http';

  if (req.method === 'GET') {

    return;

}

export function start(port = 3000): Server {

  server.listen(port, () => {
    console.log(`raw-node server listening on http://localhost:${port}`);
  });
  return server;
}

const isMain = process.argv[1] !== undefined && fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1];

if (isMain) {

# Instructions: Create TypeScript generics demo

Create the three files below. Do not modify any other files.

**Important:** `src/ts/generics/index.ts` already exists and re-exports from `./generics.js`. The new file `src/ts/generics.ts` sits **next to** the `generics/` directory, not inside it. That is fine — the two paths do not collide (`src/ts/generics.ts` vs `src/ts/generics/index.ts`). Do not touch the existing `src/ts/generics/index.ts`.

---

## `src/ts/generics.ts`

```ts
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

// --- Constrained generics ---------------------------------------------------

export function pluck<T, K extends keyof T>(items: readonly T[], key: K): T[K][] {
  return items.map((item) => item[key]);
}

// --- Conditional types ------------------------------------------------------

export type IsArray<T> = T extends readonly unknown[] ? true : false;

export type ElementOf<T> = T extends readonly (infer U)[] ? U : never;

// --- Mapped types -----------------------------------------------------------

export type ReadonlyDeep<T> = {
  readonly [K in keyof T]: T[K] extends object ? ReadonlyDeep<T[K]> : T[K];
};

export type Optional<T, K extends keyof T> = Omit<T, K> & Partial<Pick<T, K>>;

// --- Runtime helpers used by tests -----------------------------------------

export function isArray<T>(value: T): IsArray<T> {
  return (Array.isArray(value) as IsArray<T>);
}

export function firstElement<T>(items: readonly T[]): ElementOf<readonly T[]> {
  return items[0] as ElementOf<readonly T[]>;
}

// --- Demo -------------------------------------------------------------------

function main(): void {
  const people = [
    { name: 'Ada', age: 36 },
    { name: 'Alan', age: 41 },
  ];

  console.log('pluck names:', pluck(people, 'name'));
  console.log('pluck ages:', pluck(people, 'age'));

  console.log('isArray([1,2,3]):', isArray([1, 2, 3]));
  console.log('isArray("hi"):', isArray('hi'));

  console.log('firstElement([10,20,30]):', firstElement([10, 20, 30]));

  const frozen: ReadonlyDeep<{ a: { b: number } }> = { a: { b: 1 } };
  console.log('ReadonlyDeep value:', frozen.a.b);

  const partial: Optional<{ a: number; b: number }, 'b'> = { a: 1 };
  console.log('Optional value:', partial);
}

const isMain = process.argv[1] !== undefined && fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1];

if (isMain) {
  main();
}
```

Notes on the constraints:

- `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — no type-only imports needed here since everything is either a value or a locally-declared type.
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `items[0]` is `T | undefined`, so the `as ElementOf<...>` cast is required. This is a deliberate, documented cast, not an `any`.
- `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on — `Optional<T, K>` uses `Partial<Pick<T, K>>`, which is compatible.
- `isArray` uses `Array.isArray` (which narrows to `any[]`) and casts to `IsArray<T>`. The cast is safe because the conditional type is exactly what `Array.isArray` checks at runtime.
- The direct-execution check uses `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1]`, same pattern as the raw-node server.

---

## `src/ts/generics.test.ts`

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  firstElement,
  isArray,
  pluck,
  type ElementOf,
  type IsArray,
  type Optional,
  type ReadonlyDeep,
} from './generics.js';

describe('pluck', () => {
  it('extracts a key from each item', () => {
    const people = [
      { name: 'Ada', age: 36 },
      { name: 'Alan', age: 41 },
    ];
    expect(pluck(people, 'name')).toEqual(['Ada', 'Alan']);
    expect(pluck(people, 'age')).toEqual([36, 41]);
  });

  it('returns an empty array for empty input', () => {
    expect(pluck([], 'name' as never)).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('isArray', () => {
  it('returns true for arrays', () => {
    expect(isArray([1, 2, 3])).toBe(true);
  });

  it('returns false for non-arrays', () => {
    expect(isArray('hi')).toBe(false);
    expect(isArray(42)).toBe(false);
    expect(isArray({})).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('firstElement', () => {
  it('returns the first element', () => {
    expect(firstElement([10, 20, 30])).toBe(10);
  });
});

describe('type-level sanity', () => {
  it('IsArray and ElementOf resolve as expected', () => {
    const a: IsArray<number[]> = true;
    const b: IsArray<string> = false;
    const c: ElementOf<readonly number[]> = 1;
    expect(a).toBe(true);
    expect(b).toBe(false);
    expect(c).toBe(1);
  });

  it('ReadonlyDeep and Optional are usable', () => {
    const frozen: ReadonlyDeep<{ a: { b: number } }> = { a: { b: 1 } };
    const partial: Optional<{ a: number; b: number }, 'b'> = { a: 1 };
    expect(frozen.a.b).toBe(1);
    expect(partial).toEqual({ a: 1 });
  });
});
```

Notes:

- The `pluck([], 'name' as never)` call is a workaround for `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` + empty-array inference. It is a test-only cast, not production code.
- The `type-level sanity` block uses assignments to force the compiler to check the conditional and mapped types. If the types are wrong, `tsc --noEmit` fails — that is the point.

src/ts/generics.ts
````
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

// --- Constrained generics ---------------------------------------------------

export function pluck<T, K extends keyof T>(items: readonly T[], key: K): T[K][] {
  return items.map((item) => item[key]);
}

// --- Conditional types ------------------------------------------------------

export type IsArray<T> = T extends readonly unknown[] ? true : false;

export type ElementOf<T> = T extends readonly (infer U)[] ? U : never;

// --- Mapped types -----------------------------------------------------------

export type ReadonlyDeep<T> = {
  readonly [K in keyof T]: T[K] extends object ? ReadonlyDeep<T[K]> : T[K];
};

export type Optional<T, K extends keyof T> = Omit<T, K> & Partial<Pick<T, K>>;

// --- Runtime helpers used by tests -----------------------------------------

export function isArray<T>(value: T): IsArray<T> {
  return Array.isArray(value) as IsArray<T>;
}

export function firstElement<T>(items: readonly T[]): ElementOf<readonly T[]> {
  return items[0] as ElementOf<readonly T[]>;
}

// --- Demo -------------------------------------------------------------------

function main(): void {
  const people = [
    { name: 'Ada', age: 36 },
    { name: 'Alan', age: 41 },
  ];

  console.log('pluck names:', pluck(people, 'name'));
  console.log('pluck ages:', pluck(people, 'age'));

  console.log('isArray([1,2,3]):', isArray([1, 2, 3]));
  console.log('isArray("hi"):', isArray('hi'));

  console.log('firstElement([10,20,30]):', firstElement([10, 20, 30]));

  const frozen: ReadonlyDeep<{ a: { b: number } }> = { a: { b: 1 } };
  console.log('ReadonlyDeep value:', frozen.a.b);

  const partial: Optional<{ a: number; b: number }, 'b'> = { a: 1 };
  console.log('Optional value:', partial);
}

const isMain = process.argv[1] !== undefined && fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1];

if (isMain) {
  main();
}
````

src/ts/generics.test.ts
````
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import {
  firstElement,
  isArray,
  pluck,
  type ElementOf,
  type IsArray,
  type Optional,
  type ReadonlyDeep,
} from './generics.js';

describe('pluck', () => {
  it('extracts a key from each item', () => {
    const people = [
      { name: 'Ada', age: 36 },
      { name: 'Alan', age: 41 },
    ];
    expect(pluck(people, 'name')).toEqual(['Ada', 'Alan']);
    expect(pluck(people, 'age')).toEqual([36, 41]);
  });

  it('returns an empty array for empty input', () => {
    const empty: { name: string }[] = [];
    expect(pluck(empty, 'name')).toEqual([]);
  });
});

describe('isArray', () => {
  it('returns true for arrays', () => {
    expect(isArray([1, 2, 3])).toBe(true);
  });

  it('returns false for non-arrays', () => {
    expect(isArray('hi')).toBe(false);
    expect(isArray(42)).toBe(false);
    expect(isArray({})).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('firstElement', () => {
  it('returns the first element', () => {
    expect(firstElement([10, 20, 30])).toBe(10);
  });
});

describe('type-level sanity', () => {
  it('IsArray and ElementOf resolve as expected', () => {
    const a: IsArray<number[]> = true;
    const b: IsArray<string> = false;
    const c: ElementOf<readonly number[]> = 1;
    expect(a).toBe(true);
    expect(b).toBe(false);
    expect(c).toBe(1);
  });

  it('ReadonlyDeep and Optional are usable', () => {
    const frozen: ReadonlyDeep<{ a: { b: number } }> = { a: { b: 1 } };
    const partial: Optional<{ a: number; b: number }, 'b'> = { a: 1 };
    expect(frozen.a.b).toBe(1);
    expect(partial).toEqual({ a: 1 });
  });
});
````

#### Create a UDP echo client/server example.  
####   
#### - src/network/udp-echo/server.ts — binds UDP to 127.0.0.1:41234, echoes  
####   each datagram back prefixed with "echo: ". Guard with direct-exec check.  
#### - src/network/udp-echo/client.ts — sends one datagram, prints reply,  
####   exits.  
#### - src/network/udp-echo/README.md — two-line run instructions for server  
####   and client in two terminals.  

# Instructions: Create UDP echo example

Create the three files below. Do not modify any other files.

---

## `src/network/udp-echo/server.ts`

```ts
import { createSocket } from 'node:dgram';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

const HOST = '127.0.0.1';
const PORT = 41234;

export function start(): void {
  const server = createSocket('udp4');

  server.on('message', (msg, rinfo) => {
    const reply = `echo: ${msg.toString()}`;
    server.send(reply, rinfo.port, rinfo.address, (err) => {
      if (err) {
        console.error('send error:', err);
      }
    });
  });

  server.on('error', (err) => {
    console.error('server error:', err);
    server.close();
  });

  server.bind(PORT, HOST, () => {
    console.log(`udp echo server listening on udp://${HOST}:${PORT}`);
  });
}

const isMain = process.argv[1] !== undefined && fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1];

if (isMain) {
  start();
}
```

Notes on the constraints:

- `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — no type-only imports needed here.
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `process.argv[1]` is `string | undefined`, so the `!== undefined` guard is required.
- `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on — the `send` callback's `err` is `Error | null`, handled with a truthy check.
- Direct-execution check uses `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) === process.argv[1]`, same pattern as the raw-node server.

---

## `src/network/udp-echo/client.ts`

```ts
import { createSocket } from 'node:dgram';

const HOST = '127.0.0.1';
const PORT = 41234;

const client = createSocket('udp4');

const message = Buffer.from('hello udp');

client.on('message', (msg) => {
  console.log('reply:', msg.toString());
  client.close();
});

client.on('error', (err) => {
  console.error('client error:', err);
  client.close();
});

client.send(message, PORT, HOST, (err) => {
  if (err) {
    console.error('send error:', err);
    client.close();
  }
});
```

Notes:

- The client is a one-shot script — no `start()` export, no direct-exec guard needed. It runs on import, which is fine for a demo entrypoint (per CONVENTIONS.md, `src/network/` is demo-only and does not get tests).
- `client.close()` in the `message` handler ensures the process exits after the reply arrives.

---

## `src/network/udp-echo/README.md`

```markdown

Terminal 1: `npx tsx src/network/udp-echo/server.ts`
Terminal 2: `npx tsx src/network/udp-echo/client.ts`
```

#### if node:sqlite is unavailable — pick one and state your choice in the README).  
####   
#### - src/db/sqlite/init.sql — create table notes(id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,  
####   body TEXT NOT NULL, created_at TEXT NOT NULL).  
#### - src/db/sqlite/repository.ts — typed insert/list functions.  
#### - src/db/sqlite/demo.ts — runnable demo: insert 3 notes, list them.

# Instructions: Create SQLite example

**Choice: `node:sqlite`.** Node 20+ ships `node:sqlite` (stable in Node 22, experimental in Node 20/21 behind `--experimental-sqlite`). Since `engines` is `>=20` and the README already targets Node 20, use `node:sqlite` and document the flag requirement in the README. No new dependency needed.

Create the four files below, and append one line to `.gitignore`.

---

## `src/db/sqlite/init.sql`

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notes (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  body TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL
);
```

---

## `src/db/sqlite/repository.ts`

```ts
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';

export interface Note {
  id: number;
  body: string;
  createdAt: string;
}

interface NoteRow {
  id: number;
  body: string;
  created_at: string;
}

export class NotesRepository {
  private readonly db: DatabaseSync;

  constructor(db: DatabaseSync) {
    this.db = db;
  }

  insert(body: string): Note {
    const createdAt = new Date().toISOString();
    const stmt = this.db.prepare('INSERT INTO notes (body, created_at) VALUES (?, ?)');
    const result = stmt.run(body, createdAt);
    const id = Number(result.lastInsertRowid);
    return { id, body, createdAt };
  }

  list(): Note[] {
    const stmt = this.db.prepare('SELECT id, body, created_at FROM notes ORDER BY id');
    const rows = stmt.all() as NoteRow[];
    return rows.map((row) => ({
      id: row.id,
      body: row.body,
      createdAt: row.created_at,
    }));
  }
}
```

Notes on the constraints:

- `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — `DatabaseSync` is a value (class), so a regular import is correct.
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `stmt.all()` returns `unknown[]`; the `as NoteRow[]` cast is the documented boundary between the untyped SQLite driver and the typed repository. This is the one place a cast is acceptable (narrowing `unknown` at an I/O boundary).
- `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on — `Note` has no optional fields, so no issue.
- `lastInsertRowid` is `number | bigint` in `node:sqlite`; `Number(...)` normalizes it.

---

## `src/db/sqlite/demo.ts`

```ts
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';
import { NotesRepository } from './repository.js';

const DB_PATH = fileURLToPath(new URL('./demo.sqlite', import.meta.url));
const INIT_SQL = fileURLToPath(new URL('./init.sql', import.meta.url));

function main(): void {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(DB_PATH);
  db.exec(readFileSync(INIT_SQL, 'utf8'));

  const repo = new NotesRepository(db);

  repo.insert('first note');
  repo.insert('second note');
  repo.insert('third note');

  const notes = repo.list();
  for (const note of notes) {
    console.log(`#${note.id} [${note.createdAt}] ${note.body}`);
  }

  db.close();
}

main();
```

Notes:

- `demo.ts` is a demo entrypoint — it runs on import, no direct-exec guard needed (per CONVENTIONS.md, `src/db/` is demo-only and does not get tests).
- `readFileSync(INIT_SQL, 'utf8')` returns `string`; `db.exec` accepts it.
- `db.close()` ensures the process exits cleanly.

---

## `src/db/sqlite/README.md`

```markdown
# SQLite example

Uses Node's built-in `node:sqlite` module (no extra dependency). On Node 20/21
it is experimental and requires `--experimental-sqlite`; on Node 22+ it is stable.

Run it: `node --experimental-sqlite --import tsx src/db/sqlite/demo.ts`

The generated `demo.sqlite` file is git-ignored.
```

#### import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';

#### export interface Note {

#### export class NotesRepository {  
####   private readonly db: DatabaseSync;  
####   
####   constructor(db: DatabaseSync) {  
####     this.db = db;  
####   }  
####   
####   insert(body: string): Note {  
####     const createdAt = new Date().toISOString();  
####     const stmt = this.db.prepare('INSERT INTO notes (body, created_at) VALUES (?, ?)');  
####     const result = stmt.run(body, createdAt);  
####     const id = Number(result.lastInsertRowid);  
####     return { id, body, createdAt };  
####   }  
####   
####   list(): Note[] {  
####     const stmt = this.db.prepare('SELECT id, body, created_at FROM notes ORDER BY id');  
####     const rows = stmt.all() as NoteRow[];  
####     return rows.map((row) => ({  
####       id: row.id,  
####       body: row.body,  
####       createdAt: row.created_at,  
####     }));  
####   }  
#### }  
#### ```  
####   
#### Notes on the constraints:  
####   
#### - `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — `DatabaseSync` is a value (class), so a regular import is correct.  
#### - `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `stmt.all()` returns `unknown[]`; the `as NoteRow[]` cast is the documented boundary between the untyped SQLite driver and the typed repository. This is the one place a cast is acceptable (narrowing `unknown` at an I/O boundary).  
#### - `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` is on — `Note` has no optional fields, so no issue.  
#### - `lastInsertRowid` is `number | bigint` in `node:sqlite`; `Number(...)` normalizes it.  
####

#### - `readFileSync(INIT_SQL, 'utf8')` returns `string`; `db.exec` accepts it.  
#### - `db.close()` ensures the process exits cleanly.

src/db/sqlite/init.sql
````
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS notes (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  body TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at TEXT NOT NULL
);
````

src/db/sqlite/repository.ts
````
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';

export interface Note {
  id: number;
  body: string;
  createdAt: string;
}

interface NoteRow {
  id: number;
  body: string;
  created_at: string;
}

export class NotesRepository {
  private readonly db: DatabaseSync;

  constructor(db: DatabaseSync) {
    this.db = db;
  }

  insert(body: string): Note {
    const createdAt = new Date().toISOString();
    const stmt = this.db.prepare('INSERT INTO notes (body, created_at) VALUES (?, ?)');
    const result = stmt.run(body, createdAt);
    const id = Number(result.lastInsertRowid);
    return { id, body, createdAt };
  }

  list(): Note[] {
    const stmt = this.db.prepare('SELECT id, body, created_at FROM notes ORDER BY id');
    const rows = stmt.all() as NoteRow[];
    return rows.map((row) => ({
      id: row.id,
      body: row.body,
      createdAt: row.created_at,
    }));
  }
}
````

src/db/sqlite/demo.ts
````
import { readFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { DatabaseSync } from 'node:sqlite';
import { NotesRepository } from './repository.js';

const DB_PATH = fileURLToPath(new URL('./demo.sqlite', import.meta.url));
const INIT_SQL = fileURLToPath(new URL('./init.sql', import.meta.url));

function main(): void {
  const db = new DatabaseSync(DB_PATH);
  db.exec(readFileSync(INIT_SQL, 'utf8'));

  const repo = new NotesRepository(db);

  repo.insert('first note');
  repo.insert('second note');
  repo.insert('third note');

  const notes = repo.list();
  for (const note of notes) {
    console.log(`#${note.id} [${note.createdAt}] ${note.body}`);
  }

  db.close();
}

main();
````

src/db/sqlite/README.md
````
# SQLite example

Uses Node's built-in `node:sqlite` module (no extra dependency). On Node 20/21
it is experimental and requires `--experimental-sqlite`; on Node 22+ it is stable.

Run it: `node --experimental-sqlite --import tsx src/db/sqlite/demo.ts`

The generated `demo.sqlite` file is git-ignored.
````
