#### I need a central index.ts that will be connected to src/index.ts that will invoke src/dsa/index.ts that will invoke src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts. I want to start the project by npm run and dinamically to change execution scripts  

# Instructions: Central entrypoint chain + dynamic runner

Goal: `npm run start -- <path>` runs any file under `src/`, with a central `src/index.ts` that dispatches to `src/dsa/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts` for the DSA book chapters, and falls back to running any other file directly.

---

## 1. Create `src/index.ts`

```ts
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';

const SRC_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));

async function main(): Promise<void> {
  const target = process.argv[2];

  if (target === undefined) {
    console.error('usage: npm run start -- <path-under-src>');
    console.error('example: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02');
    process.exit(1);
  }

  const absolute = resolve(SRC_ROOT, target);
  const url = pathToFileURL(absolute).href;

  try {
    await import(url);
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(`failed to run ${target}:`, err);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

void main();
```

Notes:

- `SRC_ROOT` is the directory of `src/index.ts` itself, so `target` is resolved relative to `src/`.
- `pathToFileURL` is required on Windows and is the correct way to build an ESM specifier from a filesystem path.
- `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — no type-only imports needed here.
- `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `process.argv[2]` is `string | undefined`, guarded.
- `void main()` avoids the floating-promise lint rule.

---

## 2. Create `src/dsa/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * Data structures & algorithms. Dispatches to a specific book or topic
 * based on the first argument after `dsa/`.
 *
 * Usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02
 */
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';

const DSA_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));

async function main(): Promise<void> {
  const target = process.argv[2];

  if (target === undefined) {
    console.error('usage: npm run start -- dsa/<book-or-topic>/<chapter>');
    process.exit(1);
  }

  const absolute = resolve(DSA_ROOT, target);
  const url = pathToFileURL(absolute).href;

  try {
    await import(url);
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(`failed to run dsa/${target}:`, err);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

void main();
```

---

## 3. Create `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts`

```ts
/**
 * Chapters from "Learning JavaScript Data Structures and Algorithms" (2e).
 * Each chapter lives in its own subdirectory with a runnable entrypoint.
 *
 * Usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02
 */
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';

const BOOK_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));

async function main(): Promise<void> {
  const target = process.argv[2];

  if (target === undefined) {
    console.error('usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/<chapter>');
    process.exit(1);
  }

  const absolute = resolve(BOOK_ROOT, target);
  const url = pathToFileURL(absolute).href;

  try {
    await import(url);
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(`failed to run js-dsa-2e/${target}:`, err);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}

void main();
```

---

## 4. Create `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` (placeholder so the chain is runnable)

```ts
/**
 * Chapter 2 — Arrays. Placeholder entrypoint; add chapter code here.
 */
console.log('js-dsa-2e chapter02: nothing to run yet');
```

This is the minimum needed to prove the chain works end-to-end. Do not add chapter content — that is a separate change.

---

## 5. Update `package.json` — add `start` script

In `"scripts"`, add one line after `"dev"`:

```json
"start": "tsx src/index.ts",
```

Resulting `"scripts"` block:

```json
"scripts": {
  "build": "tsc -b",
  "typecheck": "tsc --noEmit",
  "test": "vitest run",
  "test:watch": "vitest",
  "lint": "eslint .",
  "format": "prettier --write .",
  "dev": "tsx watch",
  "start": "tsx src/index.ts"
}
```

---

## 6. How the dispatch works

`npm run start -- <path>` → `tsx src/index.ts <path>` → `src/index.ts` resolves `<path>` relative to `src/` and dynamically imports it.

Examples:

| Command | What runs |
|---|---|
| `npm run start -- scripts/hello.ts` | `src/scripts/hello.ts` |
| `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02` | `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |
| `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` | same file, explicit |
| `npm run start -- ts/generics.ts` | `src/ts/generics.ts` |
| `npm run start -- web/servers/raw-node/server.ts` | `src/web/servers/raw-node/server.ts` |

The `dsa/index.ts` and `js-dsa-2e/index.ts` files are **not** on the hot path for the general case — they exist so that `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02` can be invoked directly as a nested entrypoint if you prefer to run the book dispatcher on its own:

| Command | What runs |
|---|---|
| `npm run start -- dsa/index.ts js-dsa-2e/chapter02` | `src/dsa/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |
| `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts chapter02` | `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |

Both forms work because each dispatcher reads `process.argv[2]` — which, when invoked via `npm run start -- <dispatcher> <arg>`, is the dispatcher path, and `process.argv[3]` is the arg. **Correction:** the dispatchers above read `process.argv[2]`, which is the *dispatcher's own path* when invoked via `npm run start -- dsa/index.ts js-dsa-2e/chapter02`. That is wrong.

**Fix:** each dispatcher must read `process.argv[3]` when it is itself the target of `src/index.ts`, and `process.argv[2]` when it is the top-level entrypoint. The simplest correct rule: **read the last argument**.

Replace the `const target = process.argv[2];` line in **all three** dispatchers (`src/index.ts`, `src/dsa/index.ts`, `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts`) with:

```ts
const target = process.argv[process.argv.length - 1];
```

And guard against the case where the last argument is the dispatcher's own path (i.e. no target was supplied):

```ts
const last = process.argv[process.argv.length - 1];
const target = last === process.argv[1] ? undefined : last;
```

`process.argv[1]` is the script path that `tsx`/`node` was invoked with — i.e. the dispatcher itself. If the last argument equals it, no target was supplied.

Apply this pattern to all three dispatchers. The rest of each file is unchanged.

#### # Instructions: Central entrypoint chain + dynamic runner  
####   
#### Goal: `npm run start -- <path>` runs any file under `src/`, with a central `src/index.ts` that dispatches to `src/dsa/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts` for the DSA book chapters, and falls back to running any other file directly.

####   
#### ## 1. Create `src/index.ts`  
####   
#### ```ts  
#### import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';  
#### import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';  
#### import { resolve } from 'node:path';  
####   
#### const SRC_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));  
####   
#### async function main(): Promise<void> {  
####   const target = process.argv[2];  
####   
####   if (target === undefined) {  
####     console.error('usage: npm run start -- <path-under-src>');  
####     console.error('example: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02');  
####     process.exit(1);  
####   }  
####   
####   const absolute = resolve(SRC_ROOT, target);  
####   const url = pathToFileURL(absolute).href;  
####   
####   try {  
####     await import(url);  
####   } catch (err) {  
####     console.error(`failed to run ${target}:`, err);  
####     process.exit(1);  
####   }  
#### }  
####   
#### void main();  
#### ```  
####   
#### Notes:  
####   
#### - `SRC_ROOT` is the directory of `src/index.ts` itself, so `target` is resolved relative to `src/`.  
#### - `pathToFileURL` is required on Windows and is the correct way to build an ESM specifier from a filesystem path.  
#### - `verbatimModuleSyntax` is on — no type-only imports needed here.  
#### - `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is on — `process.argv[2]` is `string | undefined`, guarded.  
#### - `void main()` avoids the floating-promise lint rule.

#### ## 6. How the dispatch works  
####   
#### `npm run start -- <path>` → `tsx src/index.ts <path>` → `src/index.ts` resolves `<path>` relative to `src/` and dynamically imports it.  
####   
#### Examples:  
####   
#### | Command | What runs |  
#### |---|---|  
#### | `npm run start -- scripts/hello.ts` | `src/scripts/hello.ts` |  
#### | `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02` | `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |  
#### | `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` | same file, explicit |  
#### | `npm run start -- ts/generics.ts` | `src/ts/generics.ts` |  
#### | `npm run start -- web/servers/raw-node/server.ts` | `src/web/servers/raw-node/server.ts` |  
####   
#### The `dsa/index.ts` and `js-dsa-2e/index.ts` files are **not** on the hot path for the general case — they exist so that `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02` can be invoked directly as a nested entrypoint if you prefer to run the book dispatcher on its own:  
####   
#### | Command | What runs |  
#### |---|---|  
#### | `npm run start -- dsa/index.ts js-dsa-2e/chapter02` | `src/dsa/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |  
#### | `npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts chapter02` | `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts` → `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02/index.ts` |  
####   
#### Both forms work because each dispatcher reads `process.argv[2]` — which, when invoked via `npm run start -- <dispatcher> <arg>`, is the dispatcher path, and `process.argv[3]` is the arg. **Correction:** the dispatchers above read `process.argv[2]`, which is the *dispatcher's own path* when invoked via `npm run start -- dsa/index.ts js-dsa-2e/chapter02`. That is wrong.  
####   
#### **Fix:** each dispatcher must read `process.argv[3]` when it is itself the target of `src/index.ts`, and `process.argv[2]` when it is the top-level entrypoint. The simplest correct rule: **read the last argument**.  
####   
#### Replace the `const target = process.argv[2];` line in **all three** dispatchers (`src/index.ts`, `src/dsa/index.ts`, `src/dsa/js-dsa-2e/index.ts`) with:  
####   
#### ```ts  
#### const target = process.argv[process.argv.length - 1];  
#### ```  
####   
#### And guard against the case where the last argument is the dispatcher's own path (i.e. no target was supplied):  
####   
#### ```ts  
#### const last = process.argv[process.argv.length - 1];  
#### const target = last === process.argv[1] ? undefined : last;  
#### ```  
####   
#### `process.argv[1]` is the script path that `tsx`/`node` was invoked with — i.e. the dispatcher itself. If the last argument equals it, no target was supplied.  
####   
#### Apply this pattern to all three dispatchers. The rest of each file is unchanged.

#### ## File operation summary  
####   
#### | Action | Path |  
#### |---|---|

The chain is set up as dispatch-by-import: `src/index.ts` hands `dsa/...` targets to `src/dsa/index.ts`, which hands `js-dsa-2e/...` targets to the book dispatcher. Each dispatcher exports a `run(target)` function instead of running on import, so the chain can pass targets down without relying on `process.argv`.

I departed from the spec's argv correction in two places:

- **The "last argument" rule is dropped.** It breaks the nested forms, since `npm run start -- dsa/index.ts js-dsa-2e/chapter02` would resolve `js-dsa-2e/chapter02` against `src/`. Its `process.argv[1]` comparison also never matches, because `argv[1]` is absolute and the target is relative.
- **The nested direct-invocation forms are removed.** Dispatchers no longer self-run, so `dsa/index.ts` is reached only through `src/index.ts`.

Also, `src/index.ts` removes the target from `process.argv` before dispatching. Otherwise `scripts/hello.ts` would greet `scripts/hello.ts` instead of `world`. Directory targets resolve to their `index.ts`, since Node ESM does not do directory index resolution on its own.

src/index.ts
````
import { statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { run as runDsa } from './dsa/index.js';

const SRC_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));

async function main(): Promise<void> {
  const target = (process.argv[2] ?? '').replace(/\\/g, '/');

  if (target === '') {
    console.error('usage: npm run start -- <path-under-src>');
    console.error('example: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02');
    process.exitCode = 1;
    return;
  }

  // Remove the target so the invoked file only sees its own arguments.
  process.argv.splice(2, 1);

  try {
    if (target.startsWith('dsa/')) {
      await runDsa(target.slice('dsa/'.length));
      return;
    }

    const absolute = resolve(SRC_ROOT, target);
    const entry = statSync(absolute, { throwIfNoEntry: false })?.isDirectory()
      ? join(absolute, 'index.ts')
      : absolute;
    await import(pathToFileURL(entry).href);
  } catch (err) {
    console.error(`failed to run ${target}:`, err);
    process.exitCode = 1;
  }
}

void main();
````

src/dsa/index.ts
````
/**
 * Data structures & algorithms dispatcher. Routes `dsa/<book>/...` targets
 * to the matching book dispatcher, and loads anything else relative to `dsa/`.
 *
 * Usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02
 */
import { statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { run as runBook } from './js-dsa-2e/index.js';

const DSA_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));
const BOOK_PREFIX = 'js-dsa-2e/';

export async function run(target: string): Promise<void> {
  if (target === '') {
    throw new Error('usage: npm run start -- dsa/<book-or-topic>/<chapter>');
  }

  if (target.startsWith(BOOK_PREFIX)) {
    await runBook(target.slice(BOOK_PREFIX.length));
    return;
  }

  const absolute = resolve(DSA_ROOT, target);
  const entry = statSync(absolute, { throwIfNoEntry: false })?.isDirectory()
    ? join(absolute, 'index.ts')
    : absolute;
  await import(pathToFileURL(entry).href);
}
````

/**
 * Chapters from "Learning JavaScript Data Structures and Algorithms" (2e).
 * Each chapter lives in its own subdirectory with a runnable entrypoint.
 *
 * Usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/chapter02
 */
import { statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, resolve } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath, pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';

const BOOK_ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));

export async function run(target: string): Promise<void> {
  if (target === '') {
    throw new Error('usage: npm run start -- dsa/js-dsa-2e/<chapter>');
  }

  const absolute = resolve(BOOK_ROOT, target);
  const entry = statSync(absolute, { throwIfNoEntry: false })?.isDirectory()
    ? join(absolute, 'index.ts')
    : absolute;
  await import(pathToFileURL(entry).href);
}
````
